//! Timing-critical maintenance and bulk catch-up share bounded engine slices.
use super::{Shared, lock, refusal_json, respond};
use serde_json::json;
use snapback4_core::RuntimeError;
use std::{sync::Arc, time::{Duration, Instant}};
use tiny_http::Request;

#[derive(Default, serde::Serialize)]
struct Work { delivery_chunks: usize, expired: u64, schedules: usize, chunks: usize, parked: usize, #[serde(skip)] fired: Vec<String> }

const WORK_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_millis(2);
const CONTENDED_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_micros(250);

// Charge consumed CPU for pacing, not time the OS already kept this thread off
// CPU. The cooperative chunk deadline still uses wall time. A failed clock
// sample falls back to the more conservative wall-time pause.
struct SliceClock { wall: Instant, cpu: Option<Duration> }
impl SliceClock {
    fn start() -> Self { Self { wall: Instant::now(), cpu: Self::cpu_time() } }
    fn cpu_time() -> Option<Duration> {
        let mut at = libc::timespec { tv_sec: 0, tv_nsec: 0 };
        if unsafe { libc::clock_gettime(libc::CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID, &mut at) } != 0 { return None; }
        Some(Duration::new(at.tv_sec.try_into().ok()?, at.tv_nsec.try_into().ok()?))
    }
    fn consumed(&self) -> Duration {
        self.cpu.zip(Self::cpu_time()).and_then(|(start, end)| end.checked_sub(start)).unwrap_or_else(|| self.wall.elapsed())
    }
}

// Detached readers do not take engine tickets. Sample OS load as well as the
// round-3 engine contention signal; cache the syscall for 100 ms. Load includes
// other tenants and trails short bursts, so this is a pacing hint, not admission.
fn host_contended() -> bool {
    use std::sync::{Mutex, OnceLock};
    static SAMPLE: Mutex<(Option<Instant>, bool)> = Mutex::new((None, false));
    static CPUS: OnceLock<usize> = OnceLock::new();
    let mut sample = lock(&SAMPLE);
    if sample.0.is_none_or(|at| at.elapsed() >= Duration::from_millis(100)) {
        let mut load = 0.0;
        let cpus = *CPUS.get_or_init(|| std::thread::available_parallelism().map_or(1, |n| n.get()));
        sample.1 = unsafe { libc::getloadavg(&mut load, 1) } == 1 && load >= cpus as f64;
        sample.0 = Some(Instant::now());
    }
    sample.1
}

fn engine_work<T>(shared: &Shared, run: impl FnOnce(&mut snapback4_core::Engine, Duration) -> T) -> T {
    let mut engine = shared.engine.background();
    lock(&shared.readers).retry_missing(&engine);
    let budget = if engine.contended() || host_contended() { CONTENDED_TIME } else { WORK_TIME };
    let result = run(&mut engine, budget);
    // Publish from this guard: a second ordinary admission would both delay
    // notification and mistake maintenance for a newly arrived request.
    shared.publish_engine(&engine);
    result
}

fn phase<T: Default>(shared: &Shared, result: Result<T, RuntimeError>, drain: bool, name: &str) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
    match result {
        Err(error) if !drain => { super::adoption::background_failure(shared, name, &error); Ok(T::default()) }
        other => other,
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — lifetimes are swept into the partition log; missed schedule boundaries fire once.
fn work(shared: &Shared, drain: bool, tick: u64) -> Result<Work, RuntimeError> {
    let mut work = Work::default();
    shared.ephemeral.sweep(shared.now())?;
    work.delivery_chunks = delivery(shared, drain)?;
    loop {
        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_view_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "view recount")?.is_none() {break;}
        work.chunks += 1;
        if !drain && work.chunks >= 500 {break;}
        std::thread::yield_now();
    }
    loop {
        let swept = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.sweep_expired_for(shared.now(), 2_000, budget)), drain, "the expiry sweep")?;
        work.expired += swept;
        if !drain || swept == 0 { break; }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — advancing a replay clock does not release its schedule hold.
    if !shared.hold_schedules {
        let fired = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedules(shared.now())), drain, "schedules")?;
        work.schedules = fired.len();
        for name in &fired { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: schedule {name} fired"); }
        work.fired = fired;
    }
    while drain || work.chunks < 500 {
        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
        let (run, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
            let run = engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget);
            (run, engine.take_native_logs())
        });
        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
        let Some(run) = phase(shared, run, drain, "job")? else { break; };
        work.chunks += 1;
        match run.state {
            snapback4_core::JobState::Done => snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} done: {} chunk{}, {} row{} written", run.name, run.chunks, if run.chunks == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, run.rows_written, if run.rows_written == 1 { "" } else { "s" }),
            snapback4_core::JobState::Parked => {
                work.parked += 1;
                if shared.personas { shared.refusals.job(&run); }
                snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} parked after {} chunks: {}", run.name, run.chunks, run.error.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
            }
            _ => {}
        }
        // FIFO engine admission hands waiting requests their turn before the
        // next chunk; yield_now alone cannot make that promise for a mutex.
    }
    if drain || tick % 5 == 0 {
        loop {
            let actions = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_actions_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the action lease sweep")?;
            let effects = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_effects_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the effect lease sweep")?;
            if actions + effects > 0 { shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine)); }
            if effects > 0 { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: {effects} effect lease{} expired; queued again", if effects == 1 { "" } else { "s" }); }
            if !drain || actions + effects == 0 { break; }
        }
    }
    if drain || tick % 60 == 0 {
        phase(shared, (|| {
            let mut sweep = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.prepare_asset_sweep_for(shared.now(), budget))?;
            sweep.remove_files()?;
            while !sweep.is_empty() { engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.finish_asset_sweep_for(&mut sweep, budget))?; }
            Ok(())
        })(), drain, "the asset sweep")?;
    }
    Ok(work)
}

fn delivery(shared: &Shared, drain: bool) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
    let mut chunks = 0;
    while drain || chunks < 500 {
        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_timeline_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "timeline delivery")?.is_none() { break; }
        chunks += 1;
    }
    Ok(chunks)
}

// @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — ordinary-priority bulk work yields
// outside every shared lock. Aged service protects finite backlogs from traffic.
const BULK_AGE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum BulkKind { Delivery, Recount, Job }
pub(super) struct State {
    next: BulkKind,
    serviced: Instant,
    resume_at: Instant,
    empty_pause: Duration,
    advancing: bool,
    // Separate clock callers (and a dev reset) may wait here; maintenance never takes this lock.
    pub(super) advance: Arc<std::sync::Mutex<()>>,
}
impl Default for State {
    fn default() -> Self {
        let now = Instant::now();
        Self { next: BulkKind::Delivery, serviced: now, resume_at: now, empty_pause: Duration::ZERO,
            advancing: false, advance: Arc::new(std::sync::Mutex::new(())) }
    }
}
impl State {
    fn aged(&self, now: Instant) -> bool { now.saturating_duration_since(self.serviced) >= BULK_AGE }
    fn eligible(&self, now: Instant, aged_only: bool) -> bool {
        !self.advancing && if aged_only { self.aged(now) } else { now >= self.resume_at }
    }
    fn completed(&mut self, now: Instant, elapsed: Duration, contended: bool, worked: bool) {
        self.next = match self.next { BulkKind::Delivery => BulkKind::Recount, BulkKind::Recount => BulkKind::Job, BulkKind::Job => BulkKind::Delivery };
        self.serviced = now;
        // Target 1/8 CPU duty under contention, charging consumed thread CPU,
        // including atomic overruns. Cap at 100 ms; aged service is independent.
        let cap = Duration::from_millis(100);
        let charged = if contended { (elapsed * 7).min(cap) } else { Duration::ZERO };
        self.empty_pause += charged;
        // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — empty kinds keep their CPU
        // charge, but repay it with the next useful slice or the empty cycle.
        // Avoid two extra scheduler wakeups for every single delivery slice.
        let pause = if worked || self.empty_pause >= cap { self.empty_pause.min(cap) } else { Duration::ZERO };
        self.empty_pause -= pause;
        // An aged slice can finish after bulk flushes empty-cycle debt. Append
        // this charge without discarding repayment still owed in that ordering.
        self.resume_at = self.resume_at.max(now) + pause;
    }

    fn empty_cycle(&mut self, now: Instant) -> Duration {
        let pause = std::mem::take(&mut self.empty_pause);
        // A timed worker may just have scheduled repayment. Append remaining
        // debt rather than overlapping (and losing) either charge.
        self.resume_at = self.resume_at.max(now) + pause;
        self.resume_at.saturating_duration_since(now)
    }
}

// Admission owns no maintenance gate. Once admitted, the engine serializes the
// cursor's read -> chunk commit -> advance; the gate only protects small state
// accesses. Ordinary work never waits for a bulk thread to enter the engine.
fn bulk_slice(shared: &Shared, aged_only: bool) -> bool {
    if !lock(&shared.maintenance).eligible(Instant::now(), aged_only) { return false; }
    engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
        let kind = {
            let state = lock(&shared.maintenance);
            if !state.eligible(Instant::now(), aged_only) { return false; }
            state.next
        };
        let started = SliceClock::start();
        let result = match kind {
            BulkKind::Delivery => engine.run_timeline_turn_for(shared.now(), budget).map(|r| r.is_some()),
            BulkKind::Recount => engine.run_view_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget).map(|r| r.is_some()),
            BulkKind::Job => {
                let run = engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget);
                for line in engine.take_native_logs() { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
                run.map(|run| {
                    if let Some(run) = &run {
                        match run.state {
                            snapback4_core::JobState::Done => snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} done: {} chunks, {} rows written", run.name, run.chunks, run.rows_written),
                            snapback4_core::JobState::Parked => {
                                if shared.personas { shared.refusals.job(run); }
                                snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} parked after {} chunks: {}", run.name, run.chunks, run.error.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
                            }
                            _ => {}
                        }
                    }
                    run.is_some()
                })
            }
        };
        lock(&shared.maintenance).completed(Instant::now(), started.consumed(), budget == CONTENDED_TIME || shared.engine.has_waiters(), result.as_ref().map_or(true, |worked| *worked));
        phase(shared, result, false, "bulk maintenance").unwrap_or(false)
    })
}

fn timely_engine_work<T: Default>(shared: &Shared, run: impl FnOnce(&mut snapback4_core::Engine, Duration) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
    let result = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
        if lock(&shared.maintenance).advancing { return Ok(T::default()); }
        run(engine, budget)
    });
    promote_aged(shared, Instant::now());
    result
}

fn timely(shared: &Shared, tick: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    phase(shared, timely_engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.sweep_expired_for(shared.now(), 2_000, budget)), false, "the expiry sweep")?;
    if !shared.hold_schedules {
        let count = timely_engine_work(shared, |engine, _| Ok(engine.schema().schedules.len()))?;
        for index in 0..count {
            // One atomic boundary per admission, never the entire schedule set.
            // Adoption between slices is harmless: the next tick visits its set.
            let fired = phase(shared, timely_engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedule_at(shared.now(), index)), false, "schedules")?;
            for name in fired { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: schedule {name} fired"); }
        }
    }
    if tick % 5 == 0 {
        phase(shared, timely_engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
            let result = engine.requeue_expired_actions_for(shared.now(), budget);
            if result.as_ref().is_ok_and(|n| *n > 0) { shared.durable_committed(engine); }
            result
        }), false, "the action lease sweep")?;
        let effects = phase(shared, timely_engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
            let result = engine.requeue_expired_effects_for(shared.now(), budget);
            if result.as_ref().is_ok_and(|n| *n > 0) { shared.durable_committed(engine); }
            result
        }), false, "the effect lease sweep")?;

It can miss future commits outside the 64-commit window, even when their rows
remain; a historical future commit can still warn after its rows were deleted.
Per commit, table metadata is limited to 4,096 bytes; at most eight table names
of at most 128 bytes each are printed, with omissions explicitly marked. Largest
lead is calculated even when table metadata is omitted. No full scan, global
maximum or unbounded retained stamp set is used. No runtime/device schema,
store format or timestamps change. If this advisory inspection fails, dev prints
"store-ahead inspection unavailable" and continues, as doctor reports inspection
failure. Ordinary store/schema admission still applies. Unparsable application
images cannot fail the inspection because it never reads them; malformed table
metadata is omitted, while an unreadable commit stamp reports unavailable.

`doctor` prints offset from wall time and the same store-ahead fact. It discovers
a persisted dev host or accepts `--url` for a disposable one; with no running
host it explicitly compares the persisted store to wall time (offset zero).
Temporary-store inspection uses a separate dev clock status endpoint on a
detached reader, keeping `/dev/status` responsive during adoption. `clock advance`
CLI and endpoint include cumulative `offsetMs` and a one-line restart reminder;
with existing application data they point to isolated testing. Maintenance
failure still reports the advanced time/offset: do not repeat an advance blindly.

The jobs recipe uses the existing `dev --fresh --port 4401`, a client URL pointing
to that port, `clock advance … --port 4401`, and `doctor --url`; stop that host and
start fresh for each offset test. This requires no new store flag. Persisted
rows are never restamped or repaired automatically. `check --store` remains a
schema-adoption check: it has no running-clock selector, and comparing to wall
time there would spuriously warn during an intentional offset test.


**2026-09-15 — W6R1c: lost settlements report the recorded outcome; action claims survive advances.**
`runWorker` includes the server's code and message when an effect settlement
loses its lease. Only the server's recorded `queued` refusal is informational
and says requeued; `sent`, `running`, `failed` and unknown outcomes still go
to `onError` (or stderr). If a handler threw and its settlement cannot be
recorded, its original error is reported separately. An explicit development
advance shifts held action leases and their delegated viewer sessions with
effect leases in one transaction before publishing the new clock. Ordinary
sessions and retry times stay put; overdue leases stay overdue and revoked
sessions are not recreated. `serve` retains its existing clock and expiry.

### 20.12.1 Wave 14 development clock windows (KIT3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. The ordinary scheduler and default clock advance
keep latest-window coalescing: production must not replay an unbounded backlog
of effects after downtime. Development tests can request `clock advance
--each-boundary` (HTTP `eachBoundary:true`) to admit every missed window in
chronological boundary order, with declaration order breaking ties. Jobs drain
in that order on the final advanced clock; their periodStart/periodEnd arguments
name historical windows, while `now` remains the final advanced time. This is backlog admission, not
a historical simulation: test period-dependent work using periodStart/periodEnd,
not now. To test job logic dependent on now at each boundary, issue separate
advances. Scheduled
actions are admitted in order at the final advanced time (their deadlines
begin at admission, never at a historical periodEnd); external workers retain
their normal async execution contract. First-seen/changed schedules only prime their current
boundary. Held schedules remain held. Boundary/job admission keeps the existing
atomic saved-boundary transaction; restart never replays an admitted boundary.

Preflight always validates report metadata, including with held/replay schedules;
the hold only suppresses boundary enumeration/admission. Under --each-boundary it enumerates at most 10,000 crossed windows in total across all schedules, before
moving time or leases. A conservative report-size estimate also refuses above
15 MiB, reserving 1 MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host's
16 MiB JSON encoder (and the existing 64 MiB CLI HTTP exchange).
Retained job summaries project only bounded fields; error previews are capped
at 1024 UTF-8 bytes plus an ellipsis, with errorTruncated and the run id. The
preflight includes the worst JSON escaping cost for that preview per schedule;
full error/args/cursor payloads are never loaded into the report.
Larger requests refuse E_CLOCK_INPUT and advise a smaller
advance; this bounds both execution and the explicit report. Each schedule's
reply includes firedWindows and skippedBoundaries (periodStart, periodEnd and,
for each skipped job window, its literal `snapback4 run <job>` command).
Coalesced advances have no crossed-window cap: they execute only the latest window
per schedule, retain the exact skippedPeriods count, and preview the first 32
skippedBoundaries per schedule with skippedBoundariesOmitted naming the remainder.
Report preflight budgets that bounded preview independently of jump distance.
Calendar schedules use calendar previous/next, never fixed-duration division.
An incomplete drain reports the already advanced now/skewMs and a refusal;
shutdown specifically refuses non-retryable E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED rather than
turning a stopped job/delivery loop into successful HTTP 200;
callers inspect it and never blindly retry an advance. Ordinary maintenance
continues queued work; `run <job>` can inspect a run and `reset --clock reset`
provides an explicit fresh-test recovery if the scenario should be restarted. The default retains
existing fired/periodStart/periodEnd fields and adds the explicit windows.

Successful dev reset, clock status and clock advance replies include `now` and
`skewMs` (served time minus sampled wall time). The existing durable timed-
disclosure floor participates in both reported now and window preflight; they
cannot describe an earlier raw process-clock value after a persisted restart.
This reports the existing authoritative floor without changing its lifetime law.
Existing offset fields remain.
Reset keeps its current time unless clock:reset was requested. Error replies
after a clock change include the same clock evidence. No serve time controls,
clock rewind, job authority or production scheduler change is introduced.

implementation at `c94694f263` received independent **READY** verdicts from
[Muse Spark 1.3](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.muse.md) and a
[fresh Codex gpt-6.1-sol session](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.codex.md).
The [identical blind brief](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.brief.md)
and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits
`e0af692817` and `74ee49d4df` correct the Expo thread's proof scope, configured browser selection
in the test harness, and guide/census assertions and custom-Home qualification scope; they add no semantic contract.

### 20.13 Wave 4: the two residuals redesigned, and their blind reviews

After 0.2.19, two lanes redesigned the §20.12 residuals narrowly. Neither brings back a reverted mechanism.

- **W4a** (§20.4) journals each web admission synchronously in localStorage before `mutate` returns. The next open admits journaled records into the IndexedDB outbox. There is no new transport, no server route and no receipt. Real Chromium `witness/navigation-immediate.mjs` passes 10 of 10; it was 8 of 10 at 0.2.19.
- **W4b** (§20.12) adds `where` to a merge header, lowered into each lane's existing metered filter. Following checks tombstones before emitting an entry. No removed id or body appears at any examined ceiling, on the server and both device stores, and the hidden/absent sweep is identical before and after.

Both families reviewed integration `b1b9fd9ec` blind; each re-check read only its own family's earlier output. Records: `llp/reviews/3000.007-agent-ergonomics-wave-4.*`.

W4R1b (GPT 2; Grok 2, 3, 5): filtered discovery lanes take eager setup; 82 examinations / 41 probes pinned; broadcast and heap cut sweeps; a merge-predicate hidden/absent sweep; the card's label |

### 20.12.2 Wave 15 clock evidence (KIT4, 2026-10-18)

Implementer: Codex. A coalesced replay command includes --port with the actual
registered development listener, including memory hosts. An each-boundary
reason names every fired window. offsetMs and clockOffsetMs report the exact
requested process offset (initial offset plus accepted advances; reset clears
it); skewMs remains the observed authoritative now minus sampled wall time.
An advance captures that now/wall pair before draining maintenance; time spent
draining cannot subtract from the reported skew.
Thus ordinary elapsed milliseconds and refused advances cannot change the
reported configured offset. The durable disclosure floor still governs now.
Neither job authority, production scheduling nor expiry semantics change.

//! A process-local development clock. The release host always reads wall time.
use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

const MAX_TIME: i64 = snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX;

pub fn input(message: impl Into<String>) -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INPUT", "clock", message).guide("clock") }
pub fn release() -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY", "clock", "clock controls require dev; serve always uses wall time").guide("clock") }

/// Isolated time tests stay inside an assigned range, or request a free port.
pub(crate) fn isolated_command() -> String {
    isolated_range(std::env::var("PORT_LO").ok().as_deref(), std::env::var("PORT_HI").ok().as_deref())
}
fn isolated_range(lo: Option<&str>, hi: Option<&str>) -> String {
    match (lo.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok()), hi.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok())) {
        (Some(lo), Some(hi)) if lo > 0 && hi >= lo => format!("dev --memory --port {lo}-{hi} within the current range"),
        _ => "dev --memory --port 0".into(),
    }
}

pub fn duration(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    if text.starts_with('-') { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
    let text = text.strip_prefix('+').unwrap_or(text);
    let split = text.bytes().position(|b| !b.is_ascii_digit()).unwrap_or(text.len());
    let (number, unit) = text.split_at(split);
    let multiplier = match unit { "ms" => 1, "s" => 1_000, "m" => 60_000, "h" => 3_600_000, "d" => 86_400_000, "w" => 604_800_000, _ => 0 };
    number.parse::<i64>().ok().and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(multiplier)).filter(|n| *n > 0 && *n <= MAX_TIME)
        .ok_or_else(|| input("expected a positive whole duration with ms, s, m, h, d or w, such as +25h"))
}

pub fn offset(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    if let Some(rest) = text.strip_prefix('-') { duration(rest).map(|n| -n) } else { duration(text) }
}

fn time(value: Option<i64>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    value.filter(|n| (0..=MAX_TIME).contains(n)).ok_or_else(|| input("time must be Unix milliseconds in 0..9007199254740991; choose a smaller time or duration"))
}

pub fn run_now(text: Option<&String>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    let now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    match text {
        None => Ok(now),
        Some(text) if text.starts_with('+') => time(now.checked_add(duration(text)?)),
        Some(text) => time(text.parse().ok()),
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Clock {
    dev: bool,
    base: i64,
    offset: i64,
    started: Instant,
    epoch: String,
    revision: u64,
    #[cfg(test)]
    frozen: bool,
}

impl Clock {
    pub fn new(dev: bool, offset: i64) -> Result<Self, Refusal> {
        if !dev && offset != 0 { return Err(release()); }
        Ok(Self { dev, offset, base: time(snapback4_core::now_ms().checked_add(offset))?, started: Instant::now(),
            epoch: snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?, revision: 0, #[cfg(test)] frozen: false })
    }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(crate) fn freeze(&mut self, now: i64) { self.base = now; self.frozen = true; }
    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 {
        #[cfg(test)] if self.frozen { return self.base; }
        if !self.dev { return snapback4_core::now_ms(); }
        self.base.saturating_add(self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64).min(MAX_TIME)
    }
    /// Identifies samples taken before/after a restart or deliberate clock jump.
    pub fn offset(&self) -> i64 { self.offset }
    pub fn revision(&self) -> String { format!("{}:{}", self.epoch, self.revision) }
    pub fn presence_now(&self) -> i64 {
        // Production grace is elapsed real time, unaffected by wall-clock corrections.
        if self.dev { self.now() } else { self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64 }
    }
    /// Back to wall time: the offset `--clock-offset` and every advance added
    /// is dropped (a development reset's `{"clock":"reset"}`).
    pub fn reset(&mut self) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
        self.revision += 1;
        self.offset = 0;
        self.base = snapback4_core::now_ms();
        self.started = Instant::now();
        #[cfg(test)] { self.frozen = false; }
        Ok(self.base)
    }
    pub fn advance(&mut self, milliseconds: i64) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
        if milliseconds <= 0 { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
        let next = time(self.now().checked_add(milliseconds))?;
        let offset = self.offset.checked_add(milliseconds).ok_or_else(|| input("cumulative clock offset overflow"))?;
        self.revision += 1;
        self.offset = offset;
        self.base = next;
        self.started = Instant::now();
        Ok(next)
    }
}

/// The control CLI only talks to a loopback dev host. It does not open its store.
pub fn command(options: &crate::verbs::Options) -> i32 {
    let result = (|| -> Result<String, String> {
        if options.flags.contains_key("release") { return Err(release().to_string()); }
        if options.positionals.len() != 2 || options.positionals[0] != "advance" {
            return Err(input(format!("usage: {}", crate::cli_flags::synopsis("clock"))).to_string());
        }
        duration(&options.positionals[1]).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let (address, instance) = crate::verbs::dev_address(options)?;
        let body = serde_json::json!({"duration":options.positionals[1],"eachBoundary":options.flags.contains_key("each-boundary")}).to_string();
        // The common HTTP/1.0 exchange is EOF-delimited and bounds replies at
        // 64 MiB, enough for the preflight's complete explicit window report.
        let (status, json) = crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange_dev(address, "/dev/clock/advance", instance.as_deref(), &body, Duration::from_secs(60)).map_err(|error| {
            let evidence = crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange(address, "GET", "/dev/status", None, "", Duration::from_secs(5)).ok()
                .map(|(_, status)| format!("now={}, skewMs={}", status["now"], status["skewMs"]))
                .unwrap_or_else(|| "clock status unavailable".into());
            format!("clock advance reply failed at {address}: {error}; time may already have advanced ({evidence}); inspect /dev/status and queued work before retrying; for an isolated dev pass --port N")
        })?;
        if let Some(denied) = json.get("denied") {
            let clock = json["message"].as_str().map(|message| format!("; time remains advanced: {message}")).unwrap_or_default();
            return Err(format!("{}: {}{clock}\n  guide: clock", denied["code"].as_str().unwrap_or("E_CLOCK_INPUT"), denied["message"].as_str().unwrap_or("clock advance failed")));
        }
        if status != 200 { return Err(format!("dev refused clock advance: {json}")); }
        Ok(json.to_string())
    })();
    match result { Ok(body) => { println!("{body}"); 0 }, Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); 1 } }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod isolated_tests {
    #[test]
    fn time_test_guidance_uses_only_valid_assigned_ranges_or_a_free_port() {
        assert_eq!(super::isolated_range(Some("24000"), Some("24009")), "dev --memory --port 24000-24009 within the current range");
        for (lo,hi) in [(None,None),(Some("0"),Some("3")),(Some("4"),Some("3")),(Some("bad"),Some("3"))] {
            assert_eq!(super::isolated_range(lo,hi), "dev --memory --port 0");
        }
    }
}

Implementer: Codex. A coalesced replay command includes --port with the actual

### 20.12.1 Wave 14 development clock windows (KIT3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. The ordinary scheduler and default clock advance
keep latest-window coalescing: production must not replay an unbounded backlog
of effects after downtime. Development tests can request `clock advance
--each-boundary` (HTTP `eachBoundary:true`) to admit every missed window in
chronological boundary order, with declaration order breaking ties. Jobs drain
in that order on the final advanced clock; their periodStart/periodEnd arguments
name historical windows, while `now` remains the final advanced time. This is backlog admission, not
a historical simulation: test period-dependent work using periodStart/periodEnd,
not now. To test job logic dependent on now at each boundary, issue separate
advances. Scheduled
actions are admitted in order at the final advanced time (their deadlines
begin at admission, never at a historical periodEnd); external workers retain
their normal async execution contract. First-seen/changed schedules only prime their current
boundary. Held schedules remain held. Boundary/job admission keeps the existing
atomic saved-boundary transaction; restart never replays an admitted boundary.

Preflight always validates report metadata, including with held/replay schedules;
the hold only suppresses boundary enumeration/admission. Under --each-boundary it enumerates at most 10,000 crossed windows in total across all schedules, before
moving time or leases. A conservative report-size estimate also refuses above
15 MiB, reserving 1 MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host's
16 MiB JSON encoder (and the existing 64 MiB CLI HTTP exchange).
Retained job summaries project only bounded fields; error previews are capped
at 1024 UTF-8 bytes plus an ellipsis, with errorTruncated and the run id. The
preflight includes the worst JSON escaping cost for that preview per schedule;
full error/args/cursor payloads are never loaded into the report.
Larger requests refuse E_CLOCK_INPUT and advise a smaller
advance; this bounds both execution and the explicit report. Each schedule's
reply includes firedWindows and skippedBoundaries (periodStart, periodEnd and,
for each skipped job window, its literal `snapback4 run <job>` command).
Coalesced advances have no crossed-window cap: they execute only the latest window
per schedule, retain the exact skippedPeriods count, and preview the first 32
skippedBoundaries per schedule with skippedBoundariesOmitted naming the remainder.
Report preflight budgets that bounded preview independently of jump distance.
Calendar schedules use calendar previous/next, never fixed-duration division.
An incomplete drain reports the already advanced now/skewMs and a refusal;
shutdown specifically refuses non-retryable E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED rather than
turning a stopped job/delivery loop into successful HTTP 200;
callers inspect it and never blindly retry an advance. Ordinary maintenance
continues queued work; `run <job>` can inspect a run and `reset --clock reset`
provides an explicit fresh-test recovery if the scenario should be restarted. The default retains
existing fired/periodStart/periodEnd fields and adds the explicit windows.

Successful dev reset, clock status and clock advance replies include `now` and
`skewMs` (served time minus sampled wall time). The existing durable timed-
disclosure floor participates in both reported now and window preflight; they
cannot describe an earlier raw process-clock value after a persisted restart.
This reports the existing authoritative floor without changing its lifetime law.
Existing offset fields remain.
Reset keeps its current time unless clock:reset was requested. Error replies
after a clock change include the same clock evidence. No serve time controls,
clock rewind, job authority or production scheduler change is introduced.

    pub fn advance_worker_leases(&mut self, milliseconds: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {

    // @ref LLP 3000.001.000#workers — keep live supervised bearers valid until their orderly clock-revision replacement.
    pub fn advance_worker_leases_at(&mut self, milliseconds: i64, before: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let supervised: bool = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' OR key LIKE 'worker:family:%')", [], |row| row.get(0))?;
        if supervised {
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _sessions SET expires_at = expires_at + ?1 WHERE expires_at > ?2 AND COALESCE(family, token_digest) IN (SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' UNION SELECT substr(key, 15) FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'worker:family:%')", [milliseconds, before])?;
        }
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _outbox SET lease_until = lease_until + ?1 WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL", [milliseconds])?;
        // Advancing the dev clock cannot extend a claim past its admitting family.
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _requests SET lease_until = CASE WHEN family IS NULL OR principal_generation IS NULL OR admitted_expires_at IS NULL THEN lease_until + ?1 ELSE MIN(lease_until + ?1, COALESCE((SELECT MAX(s.expires_at) FROM _sessions s WHERE s.principal = _requests.principal AND COALESCE(s.family, s.token_digest) = _requests.family), lease_until)) END WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL", [milliseconds])?;

        Ok(())
    }

    /// or stalled, and the attempt it took stays counted.
    pub fn requeue_expired_effects(&mut self, now: i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let changed = expire(tx.conn(), now)?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(changed)
    }

    /// A bounded maintenance pass; settlement still checks the live lease.
    pub fn requeue_expired_effects_for(&mut self, now: i64, budget: std::time::Duration) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let changed = expire_batch(tx.conn(), now, 256, budget)?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(changed)
    }

    pub fn cancel_effect(&mut self, id: &str, now: i64) -> Result<EffectRun, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let run = tx.conn().query_row(&format!("SELECT {COLUMNS} FROM _outbox WHERE id = ?1"), [id], read_run).optional()?.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_EFFECT_UNKNOWN", message: "no effect with this id is available".into() })?;
        if !run.work.policy.cancellable { return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_EFFECT_CANCELLATION", message: "this effect declaration does not allow cancellation".into() }); }
        if matches!(run.state.as_str(), "queued" | "running" | "parked") {
            tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _outbox SET state = 'failed', cancelled = 1, error = 'the effect was cancelled; external effects are not undone', error_code = 'E_EFFECT_CANCELLED', settled_at = ?2, lease_until = NULL, claimed_by = NULL, lease_digest = NULL, next_at = NULL WHERE id = ?1", params![id, now])?;
        }
        let run = tx.conn().query_row(&format!("SELECT {COLUMNS} FROM _outbox WHERE id = ?1"), [id], read_run)?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(run)

//! Bounded, read-only evidence from the store's own commit clock.
use snapback4_core::{Engine, RuntimeError};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

const SKIPPED_PREVIEW: usize = 32;
const TOLERANCE_MS: i64 = 5_000;
const RECENT_COMMITS: usize = 64;
const TABLE_BYTES: usize = 4096;
const TABLE_NAMES: usize = 8;
const REPORT_ERROR_BYTES: usize = 1024;
// Reserve one MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host encoder.
const REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET: usize = crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING - (1 << 20);

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — inspect a fixed recent commit window, never application row values.
pub fn ahead(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
    // Reverse primary-key traversal: no full scan, sort, row payloads or retained
    // historical stamp set. octet_length(column) uses SQLite record metadata;
    // unlike a text length/cast/substr, it need not load oversized payloads.
    let mut query = engine.store().conn().prepare(
        "SELECT at, CASE WHEN at > ?1 AND octet_length(touched) <= ?2 THEN CAST(touched AS BLOB) END,
         octet_length(touched) FROM _commits ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT ?3")?;
    let mut rows = query.query(rusqlite::params![now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS), TABLE_BYTES as i64, RECENT_COMMITS as i64])?;
    let mut lead = 0;
    let mut tables = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut omitted = false;
    while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
        let at: i64 = row.get(0)?;
        if at <= now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS) { continue; }
        lead = lead.max(at.saturating_sub(now));
        let length: i64 = row.get(2)?;
        if length > TABLE_BYTES as i64 { omitted = true; continue; }
        let bytes: Vec<u8> = row.get(1)?;
        match serde_json::from_slice::<Vec<String>>(&bytes) {
            Ok(names) => for name in names {
                if tables.contains(&name) { continue; }
                if tables.len() < TABLE_NAMES && name.len() <= 128 { tables.insert(name); }
                else { omitted = true; }
            },
            Err(_) => omitted = true,
        }
    }
    if lead == 0 { return Ok(None); }
    let mut names = tables.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if omitted { names.push("additional tables omitted".into()); }
    if names.is_empty() { names.push("no table names recorded".into()); }
    Ok(Some(format!(
        "N_CLOCK_STORE_AHEAD: recent commit stamps lead the current clock by up to {lead}ms (latest {RECENT_COMMITS} commits; tables: {}); use a fresh store per offset test ({}), or restart with the same --clock-offset (or dev --resume-clock) to continue",
        names.join(", "), crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())))
}

/// UTC calendar text for Unix milliseconds: schedule boundaries are UTC.
pub fn utc(ms: i64) -> String {
    let (days, rem) = (ms.div_euclid(86_400_000), ms.rem_euclid(86_400_000));
    // Civil-from-days (proleptic Gregorian), exact for the I53 range.
    let z = days + 719_468;
    let era = z.div_euclid(146_097);
    let doe = z - era * 146_097;
    let yoe = (doe - doe / 1_460 + doe / 36_524 - doe / 146_096) / 365;
    let doy = doe - (365 * yoe + yoe / 4 - yoe / 100);
    let mp = (5 * doy + 2) / 153;
    let day = doy - (153 * mp + 2) / 5 + 1;
    let month = if mp < 10 { mp + 3 } else { mp - 9 };
    let year = yoe + era * 400 + i64::from(month <= 2);
    format!("{year:04}-{month:02}-{day:02}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z", rem / 3_600_000, rem % 3_600_000 / 60_000, rem % 60_000 / 1_000)
}

/// A positive duration in the units `clock advance` accepts, rounded up to a second.
pub fn span(ms: i64) -> String {
    let seconds = (ms.max(0) + 999) / 1_000;
    let (d, h, m, s) = (seconds / 86_400, seconds % 86_400 / 3_600, seconds % 3_600 / 60, seconds % 60);
    let parts: Vec<String> = [(d, "d"), (h, "h"), (m, "m"), (s, "s")].into_iter().filter(|(n, _)| *n > 0).map(|(n, unit)| format!("{n}{unit}")).collect();
    if parts.is_empty() { "0s".into() } else { parts.join("") }
}

/// Each schedule's saved boundary, in declaration order.
pub fn saved_boundaries(engine: &Engine) -> Vec<Option<i64>> {
    engine.schema().schedules.iter()
        .map(|schedule| {
            let shape = engine.store().meta(&format!("schedule-shape:{}", schedule.name)).ok().flatten();
            if shape.as_ref().is_some_and(|shape| *shape != snapback4_core::value::digest(schedule)) { return None; }
            engine.store().meta(&format!("schedule:{}", schedule.name)).ok().flatten().and_then(|value| value.parse().ok())
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Preflight is bounded before the clock or worker leases change.
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.1 — only explicit execution enumerates all crossed windows; coalesced reports keep a bounded preview.
pub fn crossed_boundaries(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool) -> Result<Vec<(usize, i64)>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    let mut boundaries = Vec::new();
    let mut report_bytes = 0usize;
    for (index, schedule) in engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate() {
        // JSON may escape every error byte; count that bound per schedule.
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add(2048 + 6 * (REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3) + 8 * (schedule.name.len() + schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule report exceeds its byte budget; reduce the declared schedules")); }
        if held { continue; }
        let Some(old) = before.get(index).copied().flatten() else { continue; };
        let end = schedule.boundary(now);
        if old >= end { continue; }
        let periods = schedule.periods_between(old, end);
        if each_boundary && periods > (10_000 - boundaries.len()) as i64 {
            return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("--each-boundary crosses more than 10000 schedule windows; use a smaller duration or omit --each-boundary to coalesce"));
        }
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add((if each_boundary { periods as usize } else { (periods as usize).min(SKIPPED_PREVIEW + 1) }).saturating_mul(1024 + 8 * schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule window report exceeds its byte budget; use a smaller duration")); }
        if !each_boundary { continue; }
        let mut boundary = schedule.next(old);
        while boundary <= end {
            boundaries.push((index, boundary));
            boundary = schedule.next(boundary);
        }
    }
    boundaries.sort_by_key(|(index, at)| (*at, *index));
    Ok(boundaries)
}

fn period(schedule: &snapback4_core::schema::Schedule) -> String {
    schedule.describe()
}

/// What each schedule did on one clock advance, and why one did not fire.
/// The core fires each schedule at most once per advance, for the latest
/// boundary, and holds it while its saved boundary is ahead of the clock.
// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — missed boundaries fire once; this only explains them.
pub fn schedule_report(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], fired: &[String], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool, port: Option<u16>) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
    let after = saved_boundaries(engine);
    // Read only bounded summaries: job args/cursors and full error payloads
    // are irrelevant to this report and must not be loaded then replicated.
    let runs = job_summaries(engine).unwrap_or_default();
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| {
        let (old, saved) = (before.get(index).copied().flatten(), after.get(index).copied().flatten());
        let current = schedule.boundary(now);
        let next = schedule.next(current);
        let mut entry = serde_json::json!({ "name": schedule.name, "target": schedule.target, "period": period(schedule), "fired": false, "firedWindows": [], "skippedBoundaries": [], "nextBoundary": utc(next) });
        if fired.contains(&schedule.name) {
            let end = saved.unwrap_or(current);
            entry["fired"] = true.into();
            let start = schedule.previous(end);
            let window = |boundary| serde_json::json!({"periodStart": schedule.previous(boundary), "periodEnd": boundary});
            let mut windows = Vec::new();
            if each_boundary {
                if let Some(old) = old {
                    let mut at = schedule.next(old);
                    while at <= end { windows.push(window(at)); at = schedule.next(at); }
                }
            } else { windows.push(window(end)); }
            if each_boundary {
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired {} windows: {}", windows.len(), windows.iter().map(|window| format!("{} to {}", utc(window["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap()), utc(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")).into();
            }
            entry["firedWindows"] = windows.into();
            entry["periodStart"] = start.into();
            entry["periodEnd"] = end.into();
            if !each_boundary { entry["reason"] = format!("fired for the period {} to {}", utc(start), utc(end)).into(); }
            if let Some(run) = runs.get(&schedule.target) { entry["run"] = run.clone(); }
            // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar periods count boundaries, not milliseconds.
            let skipped = if each_boundary { 0 } else { old.map(|old| schedule.periods_between(old, end) - 1).unwrap_or(0) };
            if skipped > 0 {
                entry["skippedPeriods"] = skipped.into();
                let mut windows = Vec::new();
                let mut at = schedule.next(old.unwrap());
                while at < end && windows.len() < SKIPPED_PREVIEW {
                    let mut item = window(at);
                    let mut args = serde_json::Map::new();
                    if let Some(program) = engine.program(&schedule.target) {
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") { args.insert("periodStart".into(), schedule.previous(at).into()); }
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") { args.insert("periodEnd".into(), at.into()); }
                    }
                    item["command"] = format!("snapback4 run {} '{}'{}", schedule.target, serde_json::Value::Object(args), port.map(|port| format!(" --port {port}")).unwrap_or_default()).into();
                    windows.push(item); at = schedule.next(at);
                }
                entry["skippedBoundariesOmitted"] = (skipped - windows.len() as i64).into();
                entry["skippedBoundaries"] = windows.into();
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired once, for the latest period {} to {}; {skipped} earlier period{} this advance crossed (from {}) did not run: missed boundaries fire once. Run {} with periodStart/periodEnd for an earlier period",
                    utc(start), utc(end), if skipped == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, utc(old.unwrap_or(end)), schedule.target).into();
            }
        } else if held {
            entry["reason"] = "schedules are held (dev --hold-schedules or --replay); a restart without the flag resumes at the next boundary".into();
        } else if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| *saved > current) {
            let due = schedule.next(saved);
            entry["heldUntil"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["nextBoundary"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("held by the store: its saved boundary {} is ahead of the clock {} (a dev restart resets the clock offset; the store keeps the boundary). Advance {} more, restart with dev --resume-clock, or test on dev --fresh",
                utc(saved), utc(now), span(due - now)).into();
        } else if old != saved {
            entry["reason"] = format!("{} started at boundary {}: a schedule fires at the boundary after the first one it sees, next {} ({} from now)",
                if old.is_none() { "this schedule" } else { "this changed schedule" }, utc(saved.unwrap_or(current)), utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        } else {
            entry["reason"] = format!("next boundary {} ({} from now; schedule times are UTC)", utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        }
        entry
    }).collect()
}

/// The report never loads full error/argument payloads from retained jobs.
fn job_summaries(engine: &Engine) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>, RuntimeError> {
    let mut statement = engine.store().conn().prepare(
        "SELECT name, state, rows_written, substr(error, 1, ?1), octet_length(error) > ?1, id FROM _jobs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 64")?;
    let mut rows = statement.query([REPORT_ERROR_BYTES as i64])?;
    let mut summaries = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
        let name: String = row.get(0)?;
        if summaries.contains_key(&name) { continue; }
        let mut error: Option<String> = row.get(3)?;
        let truncated = row.get::<_, Option<bool>>(4)?.unwrap_or(false);
        if let Some(text) = &mut error && truncated {
            let mut end = REPORT_ERROR_BYTES.min(text.len());
            while !text.is_char_boundary(end) { end -= 1; }
            text.truncate(end); text.push('…');
        }
        summaries.insert(name, serde_json::json!({ "id":row.get::<_, String>(5)?, "state":row.get::<_, String>(1)?,
            "rowsWritten":row.get::<_, i64>(2)?, "error":error, "errorTruncated":truncated }));
    }
    Ok(summaries)
}

/// Startup lines for schedules the store holds: their saved boundary leads the clock.
pub fn held_schedules(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Vec<String> {
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().zip(saved_boundaries(engine)).filter_map(|(schedule, saved)| {
        let saved = saved.filter(|saved| *saved > schedule.boundary(now))?;
        let due = schedule.next(saved);
        Some(format!("clock     schedule {} is held until {} ({} from now): its saved boundary {} is ahead of this clock; `clock advance {}` or restart with dev --resume-clock",
            schedule.name, utc(due), span(due - now), utc(saved), span(due - now)))
    }).collect()
}

/// The dev clock offset that continues a store's own time: its newest
/// recent commit stamp (or saved schedule boundary) less wall time, never negative.
pub fn resume_offset(store: &snapback4_core::Store, wall: i64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    let commit: Option<i64> = store.conn().query_row(
        "SELECT MAX(at) FROM (SELECT at FROM _commits ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT ?1)", [RECENT_COMMITS as i64], |row| row.get(0))?;
    let mut statement = store.conn().prepare("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'schedule:%'")?;
    let boundary = statement.query_map([], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0))?.flatten().filter_map(|value| value.parse::<i64>().ok()).max();
    let latest = commit.into_iter().chain(boundary).max().unwrap_or(wall);
    // Whole seconds, rounded up, so restarting twice does not drift backwards.
    Ok((latest.saturating_sub(wall).max(0) + 999) / 1_000 * 1_000)
}

pub fn has_data(engine: &Engine) -> bool {
    engine.store().conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r)", [], |r| r.get(0)).unwrap_or(false)
}

pub fn offset_line(offset: i64, running: bool) -> String {
    format!("clock     offset from wall time: {offset:+}ms{}", if running { " (lasts until restart)" } else { " (no running dev; inspecting against wall time)" })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    fn engine() -> Engine {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table alpha:\n  at: time\n  public\ntable beta:\n  at: time\n  public\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema,
            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program), 100_000).unwrap()
    }
    fn seed(engine: &mut Engine, table: &str, commit_at: i64, row_at: i64) {
        engine.seed_at([(table.into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
            ("id", snapback4_core::Value::Id(snapback4_core::ids::mint(row_at, &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).unwrap())),
            ("at", snapback4_core::Value::Int(row_at)),
        ]))], commit_at).unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_bounds_one_large_job_error_repeated_across_many_schedules() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..500 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        assert_eq!(crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap().len(), 500);
        let fired = engine.fire_schedules(60_000).unwrap();
        let message = "🧪\n\\".repeat(30_000);
        engine.store().conn().execute("UPDATE _jobs SET error = ?1 WHERE id = (SELECT id FROM _jobs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1)", [&message]).unwrap();
        let report = schedule_report(&engine, &before, &fired, 60_000, false, false, None);
        assert_eq!(report.len(), 500);
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["errorTruncated"] == true));
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["error"].as_str().unwrap().len() <= REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3));
        assert!(serde_json::to_vec(&report).unwrap().len() < 2 << 20);
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_refuses_before_the_host_encoder_limit() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..3000 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        let refusal = crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_CLOCK_INPUT");
        assert!(refusal.message.contains("byte budget"));
        assert_eq!(before, saved_boundaries(&engine));
        assert!(REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET < crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING);
    }
    #[test]
    fn utc_text_and_spans_match_the_calendar() {
        assert_eq!(utc(0), "1970-01-01T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(951_782_400_000), "2000-02-29T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(-1), "1969-12-31T23:59:59Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(1_791_468_000_000), "2026-10-08T14:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(span(12_633_000), "3h30m33s");
        assert_eq!(span(86_400_001), "1d1s");
        assert_eq!(span(0), "0s");
    }
    #[test]
    fn resume_offset_follows_the_newest_commit_and_is_never_negative() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 0);
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 3_700_500, 1);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 3_601_000);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 9_000_000).unwrap(), 0);
    }
    #[test]
    fn commit_tolerance_and_largest_lead_ignore_future_application_values() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 100_000, 1_000_000);
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().is_none());
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 105_000, 2_000_000);
        seed(&mut engine, "beta", 107_000, 3_000_000);
        let note = ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("up to 7000ms (latest 64 commits; tables: beta)"), "{note}");
        let note = ahead(&engine, 99_999).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("up to 7001ms (latest 64 commits; tables: alpha, beta)"), "{note}");
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 102_000).unwrap().is_none());
    }
    #[test]
    fn recent_window_does_not_claim_older_commits_or_read_row_payloads() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 200_000, 200_000);
        for n in 0..RECENT_COMMITS { seed(&mut engine, "beta", 100_000, 300_000 + n as i64); }
        // Deliberately unreadable images: metadata inspection must not deserialize them.
        engine.store().conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data = 'not json'", []).unwrap();
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().is_none());
        engine.store().conn().execute("UPDATE _commits SET at = 200000, touched = ?1 WHERE seq = (SELECT MAX(seq) FROM _commits)", ["x".repeat(TABLE_BYTES + 1)]).unwrap();
        let note = ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("100000ms") && note.contains("additional tables omitted"), "{note}");
    }
}

    #[test]
    fn kit4_clock_reply_samples_skew_before_a_delayed_drain() {
        let host = Host::with_store(true, snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(),
            "use personas alice\nquery time():\n  return now\n");
        let shared = host.shared.clone();
        let (entered, resume) = shared.engine.pause_next_background(false);
        let address = host.address;
        let client = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let payload = r#"{"duration":"1m"}"#;
            super::super::tests::exchange(address, &format!("POST /dev/clock/advance HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: {}\r\n\r\n{payload}", payload.len()))
        });
        entered.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(5)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(lock(&shared.clock).offset(), 60_000);
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(500));
        resume.send(()).unwrap();
        let response = client.join().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(super::super::tests::status(&response), 200, "{response}");
        let reply = super::super::tests::body(&response);
        assert_eq!(reply["offsetMs"], 60_000);
        assert!((reply["skewMs"].as_i64().unwrap() - 60_000).abs() < 200, "{reply}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn kit3_interrupted_clock_drain_refuses_with_clock_evidence_and_queued_work() {
        const CHILD: &str = "SNAPBACK4_KIT3_DRAIN_CHILD";
        if std::env::var_os(CHILD).is_none() {
            let result = std::process::Command::new(std::env::current_exe().unwrap())
                .args(["--exact", "serve::maintenance::tests::kit3_interrupted_clock_drain_refuses_with_clock_evidence_and_queued_work", "--nocapture"])
                .env(CHILD, "1").output().unwrap();
            assert!(result.status.success(), "{}{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&result.stdout), String::from_utf8_lossy(&result.stderr));
            return;
        }
        // Signal state is isolated in this one-test child, never the suite.
        crate::lifecycle::install("dev").unwrap(); crate::lifecycle::managed_cleanup();
        let host = Host::with_store(true, snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(),
            "use personas alice\ntable rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n");
        let shared = host.shared.clone();
        let before = shared.now();
        let id = lock(&shared.engine).enqueue_job("tick", Default::default(), before).unwrap();
        let (entered, resume) = shared.engine.pause_next_background(false);
        let address = host.address;
        let client = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let payload = r#"{"duration":"1m"}"#;
            super::super::tests::exchange(address, &format!("POST /dev/clock/advance HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: {}\r\n\r\n{payload}", payload.len()))
        });
        entered.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(5)).unwrap();
        unsafe { libc::raise(libc::SIGTERM); }
        resume.send(()).unwrap();
        let response = client.join().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(super::super::tests::status(&response), 500, "{response}");
        let reply = super::super::tests::body(&response);
        assert_eq!(reply["denied"]["code"], "E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED", "{reply}");
        assert!(reply["now"].as_i64().unwrap() >= before+60_000 && reply["skewMs"].is_i64());
        assert_eq!(lock(&shared.engine).job(&id).unwrap().unwrap().state, snapback4_core::JobState::Queued);
    }

    #[test]
    fn aged_bulk_progresses_when_the_bulk_thread_never_runs() {
        let host = Host::with_store(true, snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(),
            "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public\njob catchup():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] { n: 1 }\n");
        let shared = &host.shared;
        let job = lock(&shared.engine).enqueue_job("catchup", Default::default(), shared.now()).unwrap();
        // Healthy bulk gets no extra ordinary turn before its age threshold.
        let now = Instant::now();
        lock(&shared.maintenance).serviced = now;
        promote_aged(shared, now + BULK_AGE - Duration::from_nanos(1));
        assert_eq!(lock(&shared.maintenance).next, BulkKind::Delivery);
        assert_eq!(lock(&shared.engine).job(&job).unwrap().unwrap().chunks, 0);
        // No bulk thread exists. Three aged ordinary opportunities
        // still visit every bulk class, including the queued job.
        for kind in [BulkKind::Delivery, BulkKind::Recount, BulkKind::Job] {
            assert_eq!(lock(&shared.maintenance).next, kind);
            let due = lock(&shared.maintenance).serviced + BULK_AGE;
            lock(&shared.maintenance).serviced = Instant::now() - BULK_AGE;
            promote_aged(shared, due);
        }
        assert_eq!(lock(&shared.maintenance).next, BulkKind::Delivery);
        assert_eq!(lock(&shared.engine).job(&job).unwrap().unwrap().state, snapback4_core::JobState::Done);
    }

// The proof export census, signatures, result fields and failures come from TypeScript.
import ts from 'typescript';
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';
const root=fileURLToPath(new URL('../',import.meta.url));
export function proofApi() {
  const config=ts.readConfigFile(resolve(root,'packages/snapback4/tsconfig.build.json'),ts.sys.readFile);
  const parsed=ts.parseJsonConfigFileContent(config.config,ts.sys,resolve(root,'packages/snapback4'));
  const program=ts.createProgram(parsed.fileNames,parsed.options), checker=program.getTypeChecker();
  const source=program.getSourceFile(resolve(root,'packages/snapback4/src/proof.ts'));
  const module=checker.getSymbolAtLocation(source);
  const fmt=ts.TypeFormatFlags.NoTruncation | ts.TypeFormatFlags.UseAliasDefinedOutsideCurrentScope;
  const show=type=>checker.typeToString(type,source,fmt).replace(/import\("[^"]+"\)\./g,'');
  const cell=text=>text.replace(/\|/g,'\\|').replace(/\s+/g,' ').trim();
  const fields=type=>{
    type=checker.getAwaitedType(type)??type;
    if(checker.isArrayType(type)) return `Array of ${fields(checker.getTypeArguments(type)[0])}`;
    if(type.isUnion()) return type.types.map(fields).join(' or ');
    const name=show(type);
    if(!['ProofHost','DevHost','CliResult','TestSourceResult','FixtureRow'].includes(name)) return name;
    return `{ ${checker.getPropertiesOfType(type).map(p=>`${p.name}${p.flags&ts.SymbolFlags.Optional?'?':''}: ${show(checker.getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(p,source))}`).join('; ')} }`;
  };
  const functions=[],types=[];
  for(let symbol of checker.getExportsOfModule(module).sort((a,b)=>a.name.localeCompare(b.name))) {
    const name=symbol.name;
    if(symbol.flags&ts.SymbolFlags.Alias) symbol=checker.getAliasedSymbol(symbol);
    const type=checker.getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(symbol,source), signatures=type.getCallSignatures();
    if(signatures.length){
      const failures=symbol.getJsDocTags(checker).filter(tag=>tag.name==='throws').map(tag=>tag.text?.map(part=>part.text).join('')??'').join('; ');
      if(!failures) throw Error(`proof export ${name} needs source @throws documentation`);
      functions.push(`| ${signatures.map(sig=>'`'+cell(name+checker.signatureToString(sig,source,fmt))+'`').join('<br>')} | \`${cell(fields(checker.getReturnTypeOfSignature(signatures[0])))}\` | ${cell(failures)} |`);
    } else {
      const declared=checker.getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol);
      types.push(`| \`${name}\` | \`${cell(fields(declared)===''+name ? checker.getPropertiesOfType(declared).map(p=>`${p.name}${p.flags&ts.SymbolFlags.Optional?'?':''}: ${show(checker.getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(p,source))}`).join('; ') : fields(declared))}\` |`);
    }
  }
  return '## Proof API (generated from source)\n\nEvery runtime and type export of `snapback4/proof` is listed below. Signatures, result fields and failure reasons are generated from TypeScript declarations and their @throws comments; qualification checks this table against the source. Playwright types such as Page and Locator come from playwright-core.\n\n| Signature | Result / fields | Failure reasons |\n|---|---|---|\n'+functions.join('\n')+'\n\n| Exported type | Fields |\n|---|---|\n'+types.join('\n')+'\n';
}

//! FIFO admission to the single writer: a chunk cannot overtake a waiting request.
use std::{collections::BTreeMap, ops::{Deref, DerefMut}, sync::{Arc, Condvar, LockResult, Mutex, MutexGuard, PoisonError}};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

const REQUEST_QUIET: Duration = Duration::from_millis(1);
const MAX_DEFER: Duration = Duration::from_millis(5);

pub trait Clock { fn now(&self) -> Instant; }
pub struct SystemClock;
impl Clock for SystemClock { fn now(&self) -> Instant { Instant::now() } }

// @ref LLP 3000.036#6-idle-live-clients-must-not-cost-a-thread — thousands of held polls queue here; a release wakes only the next ticket.
/// Each queued ticket parks on its own condvar. One shared condvar with
/// notify_all woke every queued thread per release: quadratic wakeups when a
/// thousand change polls arrive at once.
#[derive(Default)]
struct Queue { next: u64, serving: u64, last_request: Option<Instant>, parked: BTreeMap<u64, Arc<Condvar>> }

// @ref LLP 3000.013#4-following-without-caps — background delivery yields the engine between commits.
/// Both requests and maintenance take tickets. Later requests cannot starve
/// maintenance either; every holder does one bounded operation before release.
pub struct FairMutex<T, C: Clock = SystemClock> {
    clock: C,
    engine_timing: bool,
    queue: Mutex<Queue>,
    available: Condvar,
    value: Mutex<T>,
    #[cfg(test)]
    background_pause: Mutex<Option<(bool, std::sync::mpsc::Sender<()>, std::sync::mpsc::Receiver<()>)>>,
}

impl<T> FairMutex<T> {
    pub fn new(value: T) -> Self {
        Self::with_clock(value, SystemClock)
    }
    /// FIFO coordination alone owns no engine/reader and must not create an
    /// engine span around clock drains, filesystem work or nested admissions.
    pub fn gate(value: T) -> Self {
        let mut gate = Self::new(value);
        gate.engine_timing = false;
        gate
    }
}
impl<T, C: Clock> FairMutex<T, C> {
    fn with_clock(value: T, clock: C) -> Self {
        Self { clock, engine_timing: true, queue: Mutex::new(Queue::default()), available: Condvar::new(), value: Mutex::new(value), #[cfg(test)] background_pause: Mutex::new(None) }
    }
    fn background_wait(&self, queue: &Queue, started: Instant) -> Option<Duration> {
        // An existing queue is sustained contention: join its tail now.
        if queue.next.wrapping_sub(queue.serving) > 1 { return None; }
        let now = self.clock.now();
        let quiet = queue.last_request.map_or(Duration::ZERO, |at| REQUEST_QUIET.saturating_sub(now.saturating_duration_since(at)));
        if queue.next == queue.serving && quiet.is_zero() { return None; }
        let remaining = MAX_DEFER.saturating_sub(now.saturating_duration_since(started));
        if remaining.is_zero() { None }
        else { Some(if quiet.is_zero() { remaining } else { quiet.min(remaining) }) }
    }
    pub fn lock(&self) -> LockResult<FairGuard<'_, T, C>> {
        self.admit(false)
    }
    /// Coalesce a short request burst before entering the FIFO. At the deadline
    /// maintenance takes a ticket even under continuous traffic; later arrivals
    /// cannot starve it. No engine lock is held during the deferral.
    pub fn background(&self) -> FairGuard<'_, T, C> {
        #[cfg(test)]
        let pause = self.background_pause.lock().unwrap().take();
        #[cfg(test)]
        if let Some((false, entered, resume)) = &pause { entered.send(()).unwrap(); resume.recv().unwrap(); }
        let guard = recover(self.admit(true), || self.clear_poison());
        #[cfg(test)]
        if let Some((true, entered, resume)) = pause { entered.send(()).unwrap(); resume.recv().unwrap(); }
        guard
    }
    fn admit(&self, background: bool) -> LockResult<FairGuard<'_, T, C>> {
        let trace_start = super::trace::begin();
        let started = self.clock.now();
        let mut queue = self.queue.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner);
        if background {
            while let Some(wait) = self.background_wait(&queue, started) {
                queue = self.available.wait_timeout(queue, wait).unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner).0;
            }
        }
        let contended = background && (queue.next != queue.serving || queue.last_request.is_some_and(|at| self.clock.now().saturating_duration_since(at) < REQUEST_QUIET));
        let ticket = queue.next;
        queue.next = queue.next.wrapping_add(1);
        if queue.serving != ticket {
            let turn = Arc::new(Condvar::new());
            queue.parked.insert(ticket, turn.clone());
            while queue.serving != ticket {
                queue = turn.wait(queue).unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner);
            }
            queue.parked.remove(&ticket);
        }
        drop(queue);
        let value = self.value.lock();
        // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.7 — clock advances run maintenance inside the request sample too.
        super::trace::waited(trace_start, if self.engine_timing { "engine" } else { "mutex" });
        let timing = self.engine_timing.then(|| {
            super::latency::waited(started);
            super::latency::EngineSpan::new()
        });
        match value {
            Ok(value) => Ok(FairGuard { owner: self, value: Some(value), background, contended, _timing: timing }),
            Err(error) => Err(PoisonError::new(FairGuard { owner: self, value: Some(error.into_inner()), background, contended, _timing: timing })),
        }
    }
    pub fn has_waiters(&self) -> bool {
        let queue = self.queue.lock().unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner);
        queue.next.wrapping_sub(queue.serving) > 1
    }
    pub fn clear_poison(&self) { self.value.clear_poison(); }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(super) fn pause_next_background(&self, holding: bool) -> (std::sync::mpsc::Receiver<()>, std::sync::mpsc::Sender<()>) {
        let (entered, observed) = std::sync::mpsc::channel();
        let (release, resume) = std::sync::mpsc::channel();
        *self.background_pause.lock().unwrap() = Some((holding, entered, resume));
        (observed, release)
    }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(super) fn waiting(&self) -> u64 {
        let queue = self.queue.lock().unwrap();
        queue.next.wrapping_sub(queue.serving).saturating_sub(1)
    }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub fn is_poisoned(&self) -> bool { self.value.is_poisoned() }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub fn queued(&self) -> u64 { let q = self.queue.lock().unwrap(); q.next.wrapping_sub(q.serving) }
}

pub struct FairGuard<'a, T, C: Clock = SystemClock> { owner: &'a FairMutex<T, C>, value: Option<MutexGuard<'a, T>>, background: bool, contended: bool, _timing: Option<super::latency::EngineSpan> }
impl<T, C: Clock> FairGuard<'_, T, C> {
    pub fn contended(&self) -> bool { self.contended }
}
impl<T, C: Clock> Deref for FairGuard<'_, T, C> {
    type Target = T;
    fn deref(&self) -> &T { self.value.as_deref().expect("live guard") }
}
impl<T, C: Clock> DerefMut for FairGuard<'_, T, C> {
    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T { self.value.as_deref_mut().expect("live guard") }
}
